Both of these are located south of the main deity's temple in Thillai. After worshipping the Perambalam, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar then approached the Thiru Chitrambalam, where the Lord of Dance, who bestows countless universes, performs his wondrous sacred dance. 55th verse. As the Lord of infinite bliss, the great dancer, showed the anklet He wore for His dance, his inner and outer senses converged and melted in praise, and he found salvation. He praised the Lord's compassion, who offered the world the nectar of the sacred dance, with His anklet placed in the hall, not the nectar that had poison mixed in it. The Lord performs the sacred dance in the Thiru Chitrambalam as the Lord of infinite bliss, the great dancer. The Lord's dance mentioned here is characteristic of the great dance of supreme bliss. Yogis and jnanis perceive it, benefit from it, immerse themselves in it, and experience it. The Lord of Dance shows the anklet He wore for His dance. This is revered as the raised sacred foot, the Kunjitha Padam. As the saying goes, "Seek liberation at the lotus feet," the raised foot is the target for attaining liberation. When the Lord shows it, living beings can see it. Thus, as the Lord showed the anklet He wore for His dance, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar's inner and outer senses converged and melted. "Ullam" refers to the inner instruments: mind, intellect, consciousness, and ego. "Pulanam" refers to the outer senses. "Oruvazhi selluthal" means to unite. In accordance with the sacred words of Thevaram, "Unite and remember," and "Let the five great senses be captivated by the eyes," and "The joy that is not perceived by the five senses," and "The Shiva-bhoga that comes directly from consciousness, effortlessly reaching the five senses without interruption," all these points that we have previously seen come to mind here. Thus, with his inner and outer senses converging and melting, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar praises and finds salvation. The Lord bestowed two kinds of nectar. One was the nectar given to the Devas, with poison held in His throat. The other is the nectar of the sacred dance, which He bestowed upon the world by placing His sacred foot in the Thiru Perambalam. The first was bestowed upon the Devas at one time. Although it saved them from death and granted them longevity, that nectar could not prevent death and did not give them eternal immortality. The Devas could not achieve that through that nectar. But the nectar of this sacred dance, which bestows grace upon the people of the world, is the nectar that always grants living beings a state of non-birth. It is eternal nectar. That sacred dance is the dance that grows with the anklet. "Kazhal vaithu" means to plant one sacred foot. The planted sacred foot is the foundation for the liberation that comes from the dancing, raised sacred foot. Therefore, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises it as "the sacred dance that grows with the anklet." Gnana Sastram praises this as "the hidden power that resides in the planted lotus feet." The planted sacred foot acts as the obscuring power, the Tirodhana, and then facilitates the manifestation of the power of grace. There is a reason for this sacred dance performed by the Lord. It is His compassion for living beings. We have already seen Cheraman Perumal Nayanar praise this earlier, saying, "What compassion He bestows upon His devotees!" 56th verse. Immersed in the ocean of endless joy, he praised and bowed at the feet of the Lord, singing the Thiruvandhadhi of Ponvannath, which is full of grace and beautiful colors. He was like the clouds that bring seasonal rain, incomparable in his generosity, Kazharitrarivar. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises the generosity of Kazharitrarivar Nayanar in this verse as well. He praises him as "Kazharitrarivar, whose hands are incomparable in generosity, like the clouds that bring seasonal rain." This means that Kazharitrarivar Nayanar possesses hands that bestow generosity, which is superior to the clouds that pour down rain without fail at the right season. Seasonal rain, if it falls at the right time and in the right amount, is beneficial. If it doesn't fall at the right time and in the right amount, even though rain is desirable, if it falls out of season, either too early or too late, it is not only useless but also causes damage and sorrow. Similarly, clouds appear as if they will rain. Sometimes they pass without raining. The generosity of our Cheraman Perumal Nayanar is not like that. Unlike rain that causes harm if it falls out of season, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar's generosity always brings only benefit, without causing harm at any time. This rain only gives water. But Cheraman Perumal Nayanar's generosity gives everything desired, as desired. Therefore, his generosity cannot be compared to these clouds. Such is the greatness of Kazharitrarivar Nayanar, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. Such a Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, due to his insatiable desire, immersed himself in the ocean of bliss, delighted in it, and by the grace of Lord Shiva, before the Lord of Dance, he sang and praised the Thiruvandhadhi of Ponvannath, which is full of special colors, at the foot of the Thirukalittrupadi, the sacred steps before the Sabhanayagar, so that the world might lovingly praise and enjoy it. This literary work composed by Cheraman Perumal Nayanar is called Ponvannath Thiruvandhadhi. This work was compiled by Nambiyandar Nambigal Swamigal in the eleventh Thirumurai among the twelve Saiva Thirumurais. It begins with "Ponvannam," and therefore it is named Ponvannath Thiruvandhadhi. Just as "Aathichoodi" is named after its beginning, "Aathichoodi," so it is here. Andhadhi is a type of literature. The rules for Andhadhi state that the last letter, syllable, word, or line of a verse should be the first of the next verse. There are many types of Andhadhi. This is a Seyyul Andhadhi. That is, one of the last letter, syllable, word, or line of a verse becomes the first of the next verse. This work is a Kalithurai Andhadhi structured in this way. Andhadhi comes from "Antham" + "Aadhi." This is called Dhirka Sandhi in Sanskrit. We see here that the first word of the first line of the first verse of the work and the last word of the last line of the last verse form a circle, with the end becoming the beginning. The first verse begins with "Ponvannam." Similarly, we see that the hundredth verse ends with "Ponvanname." We also see the Arputhath Thiruvandhadhi, composed by Karaikkal Ammaiyar Perumatti, which has a similar structure as a Venba Andhadhi. Therefore, this Andhadhi is one of the types of Tamil literary works. Its first verse is: "Ponvannam evvannam avvanname meni polinthilangu minvannam evvannam avvanname veezh sadai vellik kundram thanvannam evvannam avvanname maalvidai thannaik kanda envannam evvannam avvanname aagiya Easanukke." This Andhadhi was composed by Cheraman Perumal Nayanar to worship the Lord of Thillai, who accepted his worship and daily let him hear the sound of His anklets. He praised the Lord's abundant grace in many ways and presented this Andhadhi at the foot of the Thirukalittrupadi, the sacred steps before the Sabhanayagar, so that the people of this world might read it and find joy. 57th verse. As he stood before the Lord and offered the garland of Tamil words, the Lord, who dances for the welfare of the celestials, as a reward for that, caused the sound of His sacred anklet, which He had raised in the golden jeweled hall, to be heard by him. As Cheraman Perumal Nayanar stood before his Lord, the Lord of Dance, and offered the Thiruvandhadhi, a garland of words in pure Tamil, the Lord, as a fitting reward for that song, caused the sound of His sacred anklet, which had risen from His sacred foot, which He had raised in the beautiful golden Thiru Ambalam, to be heard by Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. The Lord of Dance made that sound of the anklet audible to Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. It is customary for those who are sung about by poets to give rewards to the poets. Similarly, the best reward for this literary work, a garland of words, is to make a sound audible. Therefore, as sound is the reward for sound, the Lord made the sound of His sacred anklet, the sound of His sacred foot's anklet, audible to Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. That sacred anklet sound produces all supreme joys. Therefore, that is the appropriate reward.

Such is the Lord who shines with the raised sacred foot, performing the sacred dance for the celestials to worship. "Devas to live" means that the Devas, including Brahma and Vishnu, attain well-being, which means that their activities such as creation and preservation occur well. Their activities occur well only through the Lord's fivefold dance. Therefore, the Lord performs the sacred dance for the welfare of the Devas. The Lord thus made the sound of the sacred anklet audible to Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. 58th verse. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, who heard the sound of the dancing anklet, attained the great blessing of boundless joy and bliss. He worshipped the Lord with such devotion that he prostrated himself repeatedly. After worshipping, he left the temple and stayed outside, bowing to the streets of the grand mansions. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, who heard the sound of the Lord's dancing anklet, worshipped the Lord, attaining the state of great blessing where boundless joy and bliss converged. He bowed repeatedly and then left the temple, bowing to the streets of the grand mansions, and stayed outside in Thillai. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 13 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 949, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar Puranam. 59th verse. In those days, he, who resided in the revered circle of Thillai, worshipped day and night, with ever-increasing love, at the foot of the steps where the Vedas reside, as the Lord, with the serpent and water in His matted locks, danced in the golden hall, and his joy blossomed. The Thirumandiram praises Chidambaram as "everywhere Chidambaram." Periyapuranam praises it as a sacred place that fills all universes. Chidambaram is a place praised by all. Chidambaram is the place where Cheraman Perumal Nayanar always worships and praises. In that Chidambaram, in the golden sacred hall, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar continues to praise day and night, with ever-increasing love, the Lord's sacred dance, where the serpent and the Ganga reside in His matted locks and dance. No matter how much he sees and enjoys, no matter how much he praises, he remains unsatisfied. He stays at the foot of the sacred steps, praising continuously. Those sacred steps are the steps before the Sabhanayagar; they are also called Panchakshara Padi and Thirukalittrupadi. They are the steps where the Vedas reside. The Vedas praise these steps. The five letters of the Sri Panchakshara themselves form these steps, supporting the Lord's sacred feet. The Vedas praise Shiva with the five sacred letters. Therefore, these steps are praised by the Vedas. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, by bowing and praising day and night at the foot of that Thirukalittrupadi, has attained great joy. On such a day, divine grace prompts Cheraman Perumal Nayanar to travel towards Thiruvarur. 60th verse. Remembering the Lord's grace, who, having heard the songs of the dancing Lord, delayed the sound of the anklet, and then, in a fitting manner, previously granted him the friendship of Nanjavur Kovai, his mind, filled with great and enduring love, desired to see the Lord's feet, which are sought by Vishnu, and so, by the grace of the Lord, he set out for Thiruvarur. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar remembers the grace of the Lord, who, having heard the songs of the dancing Lord, delayed the sound of the anklet, and then, in a fitting manner, previously granted him the friendship of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, saying that he had delayed making the anklet sound audible to Cheraman Perumal Nayanar. His mind is filled with increasing thoughts of seeing Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the king of Thirunavalur, due to his great and enduring love for him. Therefore, by the grace of the Lord, whose feet are sought by Vishnu, he set out for Thiruvarur. 61st verse. He worshipped the boundary of Thillai, which is the limit of knowledge, and held the inseparable sacred feet in his overflowing heart. He then went to Pugali, where the abundant gnana-bhogana (one who enjoys divine knowledge) came and bestowed grace, and worshipped. He then worshipped many temples of the Lord with the deer in His hand, and joyfully continued his journey. Chidambaram is the limit of knowledge, the culmination of knowledge. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar lovingly worships the sacred boundary of Thillai, which is such a limit of knowledge. The inseparable sacred feet. Those sacred feet are the limit of beauty and art, the dancing feet. The sacred feet that dance for the welfare of the world. He holds such sacred feet in his heart, overflowing with ever-increasing love. Even though he is physically separated from Thillai, he always worships those sacred feet in his mind. Thus, worshipping the inseparable sacred feet and deeply meditating on them in his mind, he bows to the boundary of Chidambaram and begins his sacred journey towards Thiruvarur. On the way, he has a vision of the sacred place of Sirkazhi. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar worships the sacred place of Sirkazhi, where the Lord of abundant Shiva-gnana, Aludaiya Pillaiyar, Thirugnanasambandha Sivaperuman, incarnated and bestowed grace. Then he joyfully continues his journey, worshipping many temples of the Lord who holds a deer in His hand. These are the sacred places between Sirkazhi and the northern bank of the Kaveri. They are considered to be places like Thiru Pullirukkuvelur, Vaidheeswaran Koil, Thirunindriyur, and so on. Thus, traveling south from Sirkazhi, he visits all these places and then journeys towards Thiruvarur via Mayiladuthurai. 62nd verse. He crossed the Ponni River, whose waters, filled with pearls, flowed through ditches, fertile fields, sluices, and pools, and whose waves scattered pearls. He then worshipped the places beloved by the Lord with the sacred forehead eye, which shone on the path. He then reached Kazhani Arur, which was not affected by the great floods. The Ponni River. He sees the waters of the Ponni River, which flow through ditches, fertile fields, sluices, pools, and whose waves scatter pearls everywhere. The Kaveri, originating from the mountains, brings pearls, bamboo, shells, sugarcane, and rice, and deposits them in the ditches, pits, and pools along its course. Crossing such a Kaveri and ascending its southern bank, he worships all the sacred places where the Lord with the sacred forehead eye lovingly resides. These sacred places are located between Sirkazhi, the crossing point of the Kaveri, and Thiruvarur. They are Thiruvazhuvur Veerattam, Thirumiyachur, Miyachur Ilankoil, Thiruthilathaipathimuttram, Thiru Ambar Mahalam, Thiruvirkodi, and so on. After worshipping all these places, he reaches Thiruvarur. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar has a vision of Thiruvarur. Thiruvarur is a sacred place that is not affected by great floods, even during times of deluge. It is a place surrounded by fields. Thiruvarur has witnessed many deluges. We have already seen the history of the Lord who resides in the Paravaiyul Mandali temple, who protected Thiruvarur from being destroyed by the overflowing waters of the deluge. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar has a vision of such a Thiruvarur. At the same time, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal has visited places like Nagai Karonam and has arrived in Thiruvarur. 63rd verse explains this. On that day, Nambi went and sang at Nagai Karonam, and received beautiful golden ornaments, precious jewels, clothes, sandalwood paste, powerful horses, golden daggers, and other things. He then worshipped the Lord in many other sacred places and returned to Thiruvarur. We have seen the history of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in the Puranam of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar. In that, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, by divine grace, received the company of Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar, and both Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal worshipped the Lord together in Thiruvarur. After that, Eyarkon Kalikkama Nayanar returned to his own place. Then Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visited many sacred places, worshipped the Lord, and returned to Thiruvarur and stayed there again. At this time, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar arrives. The places visited by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal during that intervening period include Nagai Karonam. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal went to Nagai Karonam, sang a sacred hymn, and received beautiful golden and jeweled ornaments, precious jewels, clothes, sandalwood paste, powerful horses, golden daggers, and other things from the Lord. He then worshipped the Lord in many other sacred places and returned to Thiruvarur and stayed there. The "many other sacred places" mentioned here are the places between Nagai Karonam (which is now called Nagapattinam) and Thiruvarur. They are considered to be places like Thirukeezhvelur, Thiruchikkal, and so on. Thus, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal arrived in Thiruvarur.

At the very moment Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was residing in Thiruvarur, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, arrives in Thiruvarur, desiring to see Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 14 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 950, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar Puranam. Cheraman Peruman Nayanar reaches Thiruvarur. 64th verse. When the great Chera king arrived and reached the enduring Thiruvarur, the great Brahmin (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal), upon the arrival of the great king, went forth with a joyful heart to meet him. Cheralanar, with his fragrant garland, bowed before Vanthondar with devotion. As Cheraman Peruman Nayanar arrived at Thiruvarur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the chief of the Brahmins, went forth with a joyful heart to meet Cheraman Peruman Nayanar. Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, too, bowed before Vanthondar with devotion. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman highlights here that both Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, the great king, and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, the chief of the Brahmins, are referred to as "Peruman" (great one), indicating their equal status. The Lord had previously reminded Cheraman Peruman Nayanar of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal by the name "Vanthondar." Therefore, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, keeping that thought in mind, and having come here with the intention of seeing Vanthondar, bowed before Sundaramoorthy Swamigal with that thought. 65th verse. As the king bowed before him, he too bowed, embraced him with overflowing love, and the other, too, quickly embraced him with eagerness. Like those who drown in a flood of joy and cannot reach the shore, their bones melted, their souls united, and their bodies became one. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, seeing Cheraman Peruman Nayanar prostrate before him, also bowed, lifted him up, and embraced him with overflowing love. The other, too, quickly embraced him with great affection. They were like those who drown in a flood of joy and cannot reach the shore. Their very bones melted, their souls united within, and their two bodies became one, as if to say, they embraced each other in friendship. The union of souls is an emotional experience that is imperceptible to the eye. Here, the bodies of both become one. Only when the soul is one can the bodies become one. Therefore, they were inseparably united. We also see here a hint of the future narrative, where these two great individuals, with these very bodies, will ascend to Kailash with the Lord, their beloved. 66th verse. Seeing this state, the devotees attained boundless joy. The noble Chera king and Vanthondar, having mingled in friendship, the glorious name "Cheraman Thozhar" (Friend of Cheraman) became renowned throughout the world for the king of Munaippadi. All the devotees who witnessed them embracing each other in friendship attained boundless joy. Embracing and cherishing each other with mutual affection is characteristic of friendship. This mingling in friendship comes from divine providence. Here, this friendship arose because the Lord Himself brought it about. Therefore, due to the way Cheraman Peruman Nayanar and Nambigal, who was Vanthondar, mingled with each other, the globally renowned and sacred name "Cheraman Thozhar" was bestowed upon Sundaramoorthy Swamigal in this world. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is revered and worshipped as Cheraman Thozhar. Earlier, the Lord had declared, "We have given Our friendship to you." Therefore, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal was known by the name "Thambiran Thozhar" (Friend of the Lord). Today, seeing Nambigal and Cheranar embracing each other with equal friendship, the name "Cheraman Thozhar" was bestowed upon Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The sacred name "Cheraman Thozhar" shone with greater glory than the name "Thambiran Thozhar." This occurred due to divine grace. Moreover, this indicates that the union with devotees brings a more exalted experience of bliss than the union with the Lord.

In the Sivagnana Bodham, in the 12th Sutra, which follows the 11th Sutra that concludes with the mention of Shiva's grace, we see the state where the union with devotees is specifically discussed as "Anbarodu Maree." Although the name "Cheraman Thozhar" for Sundaramoorthy Swamigal is rarely found in Tamil Nadu, by the true divine words of Sekkizhar Peruman, we still see today that in Chera Nadu, in Thiru Anjaikkalam, when the Malayali people praise Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, they praise him by this name, "Cheraman Thozhar." Sivakavimani Desikanar has noted that when Cheraman Perumal Nayanar and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal were seated on the same seat, and the people of Malayalam country came to worship, they pointed to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and said, "Is this person the friend?" and that this brought him immense and boundless joy. 67th verse. With their hearts united in joy, they exchanged words of bliss. With the thought, "What can we do for the Lord who has brought us together in such sweet companionship?" and with the joy that arose from this, Cheraman Thozhar, holding the red hand of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, entered the temple, leaving the chariot, to worship the feet of the Lord. With the emotions that united them, they exchanged joyful words of bliss. They spoke to each other of the joyful words of divine grace. The Lord, who gives new joy with every interaction, reminded them of each other, brought them together, and created the great joy that comes from the company of devotees. With the joy that arose in their hearts, thinking, "What can we do in return for such grace of the Lord?" Nambigal, who was Cheraman Thozhar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, holding the red hand of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, left the chariot in the streets of Thiruvarur and entered the temple to worship the feet of the Lord. We see here that the idea that worshipping the Lord in the company of devotees is supreme bliss is expressed. 68th verse. He went and first worshipped the Devasiriyan, then circumambulated the sacred palace. As Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, with a united heart, entered, Nambi stood before him and worshipped. He prostrated himself on the ground, tears flowing like a waterfall from his eyes, and praised the feet of his eternally beloved Lord. As mentioned before, as Sundaramoorthy Swamigal led him by the hand, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar entered the Thiruvarur temple. First, he worshipped the Devasiriyan Mandapam located in the sacred courtyard. Then, circumambulating the Lord's sacred palace, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, with a united heart, entered. As Nambigal, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, stood before him and worshipped, he, standing behind Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, worshipped the Lord who resides in the anthill. Tears flowed like a waterfall from his eyes, and he prostrated himself on the ground, praising the feet of his eternally beloved Lord, who resides in the anthill. 69th verse. He worshipped the feet of the divine Lord, whom Devas and sages worship. The great Chera king, whose glory is boundless, then, before Nanjavur, the Nanjavur-born, made the Thirummanikkkovai, which is the foremost of the three jewels, audible to him, and the Lord Himself accepted it. The great Chera king, whose glory is boundless, worshipped the feet of the divine Lord, Thyagesa Peruman, whom Devas and sages worship. He then, before Nambigal of Thirunavalur, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, sang the Thirummanikkkovai, a literary work that praises the Lord, who is the foremost of the three, Thyagaraja Peruman, and made it audible to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Thyagaraja Peruman also accepted it and bestowed His grace. Thyagaraja Peruman is the foremost of the three. He is the foremost of Brahma, Vishnu, and Rudra. This means that He is the cause of all their activities and acts on His own. "He is Hari, the protector; He is Ayan, the creator; He is Aran, the destroyer; He is Himself the Lord," praises the Thirukailaya Gnana Ula.

Cheraman Perumal Nayanar sings the Thirummanikkkovai of Thiruvarur, praising Thyagesa Peruman, who is the foremost of the three. Thirummanikkkovai means a garland strung with three different jewels. Here, in the context of this work, the three jewels are the verses composed in the poetic forms of Asiriyappa, Venba, and Kalithurai. Although the jewels are of three types, they are strung together by a single thread, like a garland. Similarly, here, the Lord, who is the foremost of these three, is the subject of these poems. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar composed this Thirummanikkkovai of Thiruvarur so that this world may read it, benefit from it, and flourish. This Thirummanikkkovai was compiled by Nambiyandar Nambigal Swamigal in the 11th Thirumurai. This entire work is composed in the Akathurai (inner emotional) genre and praises the glories of the Lord of Thiruvarur. Among these Akathurais, this work predominantly focuses on separation and the reasons for separation, which are the main themes of the Palai Thinai (a genre of love poetry). Other genres are present in a minor way. The core idea of this Thirummanikkkovai is to praise the glories of the Lord of Thiruvarur by embedding them within the Akathurai themes. In this Thirummanikkkovai of Thiruvarur, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar praises the Lord in various ways, such as: "The Lord with knowledge as His consort, who rides a great bull; my Father, whose beautiful throat is adorned; the perfect one who bestowed a good boon upon Arjuna of infinite strength; the moon-crowned one who is beyond the boundless universe; the Lord who chose a single arrow to burn the three cities of the enemies; the Lord with knowledge as His consort, the nectar residing in Aroor; the three-eyed Lord with a fragrant garland; the Lord who destroyed the power of the one named Koothu (Yama); the Sankaran who wears a serpent in His matted locks; the primordial Lord whom even the Devas do not know; the Father of Kumaran; the Lord with cool matted locks; my Father with anklets on His feet; the Lord who holds the Ganga in one matted lock; the Lord who skinned the elephant that was bathing in muddy water; the eternal Lord; the one who gave a part of His body to the daughter of the beautiful mountain and established her with His love." We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 15 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 951, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar Puranam. Kazharitrarivar Nayanar composes the Thirummanikkkovai of Thiruvarur. 70th verse. Taking the one who arose with grace from that place, Vanthondar went out and approached the sacred mansion of Nankai Paravaiyar in Aroor. The beautiful-browed lady, having arranged lamps, full pots, flower garlands, and fragrant incense everywhere, came forward to greet him. Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, having received the Lord's grace before Thyagesa Peruman in the Thiruvarur temple, was led by Nambi Aroorar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, to the sacred mansion of Nankai Paravai Nachiyar. There, Paravai Ammaiyar greeted him by arranging lamps, full pots, flower garlands, fragrant incense, and other auspicious items everywhere. This kind of welcome for elders is our tradition. We still see this custom in Chera Nadu and Eelam today. The commentator notes that our South Tamil Nadu has forgotten this custom. Paravai Ammaiyar also came forward to greet him at the entrance of the sacred mansion. Welcoming guests who arrive at the home is one of the great duties of household life for the lady of the house. Therefore, she welcomes him. 71st verse. In the jeweled mansion, on a couch with shining golden legs, he seated the great king. Nambi sat with him. Paravaiyar, of faultless character, offered full worship to her husband and his friend, according to the righteous customs. Paravai Ammaiyar performs the worship that should be offered to devotees when they arrive at one's home, seating them on a seat. She seats Cheraman Peruman Nayanar on a couch with shining golden legs. Nambigal, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, sits with him. Nankai Paravai Nachiyar, of faultless character, offers full worship to her husband, Nambigal, and his friend, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, according to the rules of the Sivagamas, without deviating from righteous conduct. We saw this in the Thirupuranam of Ilaiyankudi Mara Nayanar: "After bringing them into the house, washing their feet, and then, with overflowing love, seating them on the seat and performing archana." Thus, Paravai Nachiyar performs devotee worship. 72nd verse. When the companion (Sundaramoorthy Swamigal) asked the great Chera king, who had leaping horses, to prepare sacred food, Paravaiyar, who was like a lamp with a stimulating flame, quickly prepared various kinds of curries and cooked rice as desired, and served a feast to all who had come with him. When Sundaramoorthy Swamigal asked his consort, Paravai Ammaiyar, who was like a lamp with a stimulating flame, to prepare sacred food for Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, the great Chera king who had leaping horses, Paravai Ammaiyar, whom the divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises in this verse with the epithet "Thoondu Jyoti Vilakkanaiyar" (like a lamp with a stimulating flame), quickly prepared various kinds of curries and cooked rice as desired. "Vilakku anaiya" (like a lamp) refers to her commitment to follow instructions. Thus, Paravai Ammaiyar is praised here as "Thoondu Jyoti Vilakkanaiyar." As Sundaramoorthy Swamigal said this, Paravai Ammaiyar quickly prepared various kinds of curries and cooked rice. She prepared a feast for all who had come with Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, according to their nature. These were food items of different tastes and types. We have seen that food has four types and six tastes. "Ponakam" refers to sacred food, cooked rice. Therefore, "veru veru vidhathu kariyum ponakamum" (various kinds of curries and cooked rice) in the verse can be interpreted as different kinds of curries along with the cooked rice, or as different kinds of cooked rice, such as various mixed rice dishes.

73rd verse. More than the special arrangements made for the king, Paravaiyar, who was like the goddess born from the ocean, prepared the desired sacred food with overflowing love, suitable for a devotee. After that, she came and informed Sundaramoorthy Swamigal that the Chera king, who had elephants with bells, and his entourage were ready to partake of the sacred food. More than the special arrangements made for Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, befitting his status as a king, Paravai Ammaiyar, with overflowing love, prepared the desired sacred food with even greater excellence, suitable for a devotee. The special arrangements made for a king are worldly. The sacred food prepared for a devotee is about the nature of love for Shiva. The latter is far superior and more excellent than the former. Both these qualities are excellent in Cheraman Peruman Nayanar. Although the excellence of being a king and the excellence of being a devotee are both great, here, Paravai Ammaiyar served sacred food based on his quality as a devotee. We also understand that this quality is the underlying principle of Periyapuranam. After preparing the sacred food in this manner, Paravai Ammaiyar informs Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. In this verse, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises Paravai Ammaiyar as "Paravai pirandha thiru anaiya Paravaiyar" (Paravaiyar, who is like the goddess born from the ocean). "Paravai" means ocean. "Thiru anaiya" means like the goddess. So, "Paravaiyar, who is like the goddess born from the ocean" is its meaning. Superficially, "Thiru pirandha thiru" (goddess born from the ocean) generally refers to Mahalakshmi, the goddess born from the milk ocean. But "Paravai pirandha thiru" here, "Paravai" refers to the ocean of grace. "Thiru" refers to Umaiyamai, who arises from the ocean of grace. This refers to Mahalakshmi, who is the embodiment of Shiva-Shakti. Such a Mahalakshmi, the embodiment of Shiva-Shakti, is the goddess worshipped in the Vayu Kona (north-west direction) in Shiva worship. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman beautifully explains in this verse the greatness of Paravai Ammaiyar, stating that she attained such a state by serving Umaiyamai and performing sacred service. 74th verse. When the renowned Vanthondar said, "O Paravai, with your flower-laden tresses, by the merit of your past penance, the great Chera king has arrived to partake of sacred food. Therefore, without delay, serve the sacred food," Paravai Ammaiyar, with her beautiful hands, arranged the plates in two separate places. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal says to Paravai Ammaiyar, "O Paravai, with your flower-laden tresses, by the merit of your past penance, we have received the great Chera king here to partake of sacred food. Therefore, without delay, serve the sacred food." Thus, the renowned Vanthondar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, instructs her. Paravai Ammaiyar, with her beautiful hands, arranges the plates in two separate places. "In two separate places" means in two different locations. She arranges plates in one place for Nambigal and in another place for Cheraman Peruman Nayanar. The Ammaiyar arranges them in this manner, according to the rules of dharma, based on the worldly status that one is a Saiva Brahmin and the other is a king. However, when Sundaramoorthy Swamigal requests Cheraman Peruman Nayanar to partake of the sacred food with him, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar initially refuses. Then, due to the nature of their love, when Sundaramoorthy Swamigal requests again, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar agrees to partake of the sacred food with him. We see here that the nature of love is superior to worldly dharma. Therefore, Paravai Ammaiyar arranges the plates accordingly. Paravai Ammaiyar arranges everything necessary for partaking of the sacred food, such as placing the seats, providing water for washing hands, and so on. 75th verse. When the Lord Nambi asked the great king to partake of the sacred food with him, the king quickly bowed in reverence. But when Nambi, holding his great hand, again earnestly requested him, the king of the Pandyas agreed to partake of the sacred food. When Aludaiya Nambigal, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, asked Cheraman Peruman Nayanar to partake of the sacred food with him, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar quickly bowed in reverence and was startled. However, when Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, holding Cheraman Peruman Nayanar's sacred hands, again earnestly requested him, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar agreed to partake of the sacred food with him. 76th verse. As they partook of the sacred food together, Paravaiyar, of elevated penance, served the sacred food with great delight. She then arranged a feast for all the attendants, with six tastes as they desired, and thus, with overflowing joy, she fulfilled the duty of a pure host. As mentioned before, when Cheraman Peruman Nayanar agreed, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar partook of the sacred food together. Paravai Ammaiyar, of elevated penance, served the sacred food with great delight. Paravai Ammaiyar's previous state of service in Kailash and her past service in Thiruvarur resulted in her receiving the great blessing of serving sacred food to Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar together here. Thus, she serves the sacred food. She also serves a feast of six tastes to the attendants, according to their needs, so that they may eat with pleasure. All the attendants are filled with great joy. Thus, Paravai Ammaiyar sweetly fulfills the duty of a pure host. Hospitality is a duty. Among the duties of household life, hospitality is the most excellent. That is why the divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to it as a "duty" here. Because she served sacred food with love for Shiva, considering the quality of being a devotee of Shiva as supreme, this becomes a pure feast. All other feasts given to worldly people are based on attachment and do not help to cut off rebirth. Therefore, they are not pure feasts. A feast given to Shiva's devotees becomes a pure feast that cuts off the bonds of rebirth. 77th verse. The women prepared and offered fragrant sandalwood paste mixed with green camphor, musk paste that brought joy, and pure, fragrant flower garlands. The golden-creeper-like women then offered sweet Panchavasam and betel nut with betel leaves. In this verse, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman explains the courtesies extended after partaking of the sacred food. They prepared sandalwood paste mixed with green camphor, saffron, rosewater, and other ingredients. The women who were to prepare this sandalwood mixture ground and prepared it. They also offered musk paste, which was made by mixing musk and other rare fragrant ingredients, and was meant to be worn as a tilak. They also offered pure flower garlands, beautifully arranged. Then, Panchavasam and betel nut with betel leaves. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman refers to this here as "Panchavasamudan adaikkai amudu." Panchavasam and betel nut with betel leaves are meant to be offered together. "Adaikkai amudu" refers to betel nut with betel leaves. "Adai" means betel leaf, and "kai" means nut. Therefore, "adaikkai amudu" is betel nut with betel leaves. So, in this verse, the fragrant mixture is for applying to the body. Musk paste and flower garlands are for wearing. Panchavasam and betel nut with betel leaves are for eating. The women assigned to this task stand ready to offer these. 78th and 79th verses. Having accepted all the worship offered with such excellence, he received the sacred ashes on his head, worshipped, and adorned himself with them. As he remained with great delight, Nambi, having attained the true nature of Kazharitrarivar's devotion, bowed at his golden feet. As the great king bowed, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, bowing in return and embracing him, praised the grace of the Lord with the Ganga in His matted locks, who had brought together the victorious king with the bow banner and Paravaiyar, whose face was like the moon, and sat with him. Having lovingly accepted all the worship offered with such excellence as mentioned before, he received the purifying sacred ashes, worshipped, and adorned himself with them on his sacred head. As he remained with great delight, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar, having truly attained the pure nature of devotion, prostrated himself at the golden feet of Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. As Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, the great king of the mountain country, bowed, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal also bowed in return and embraced him. He then praised the grace of the Lord with the Ganga in His matted locks, who had brought together Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, the victorious king with the bow banner, and Paravaiyar, whose face was like the moon, and sat with him. 80th verse. Worshipping the feet of the Lord with the mountain as His bow, who joyfully resides in the golden anthill in the prosperous Thiruvarur, and serving the grand procession of Veethividanga Peruman, both great scholars, with enduring love, lived happily day by day. Worshipping the feet of the Lord with the Meru mountain as His bow, who joyfully and lovingly resides in the golden anthill in the prosperous Thiruvarur, and serving the grand procession of Veethividanga Peruman, Thyagesa Peruman, both great scholars, with enduring love, lived happily day by day, having attained great life. The period of Cheraman Perumal Nayanar's arrival here could be during the Panguni festival or the Thiruvathirai festival. The Lord's festival is a festival that brings great life. It is a festival that bestows the ultimate reality of great life. Thus, by witnessing such a festival and worshipping the Lord of Thiruvarur, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar and Sundaramoorthy Swamigal resided in Thiruvarur with great excellence. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 16 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 952, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarukkam, Kazharitrarivar Nayanar Puranam. Cheraman Peruman Nayanar resides in Thiruvarur with Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Now, the 81st verse. As they lived in this manner, one day, Vanthondar, adorned with shining jeweled ornaments, remembered in due course to worship the many places where the Lord with the dark, shining throat resided, including Thirumadurai and other ancient cities in the Tamil country, which is called the virgin land, where the Lord with abundant matted locks resides. As they lived immersed in Shiva-bliss in this manner, worshipping the Lord of Thiruvarur and enjoying Shiva-bliss, Vanthondar, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, thought of worshipping the many places where the Lord, the Brahmin, resided. He thought of worshipping Thirumadurai and other sacred places in the Pandiya Nadu, which is called the virgin land and the Tamil country. Divine grace prompted this thought in Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal thought of visiting the sacred places in the Chola country and the Pandiya country, which are located between the Chola country and Madurai, and worshipping all the sacred places in the Pandiya Nadu, including Thirumadurai, which possesses immeasurable glories, and which is praised as the virgin land and the Tamil country. 82nd verse. The Chera king, due to his inseparable attachment to Aroor and his increasing love, and also due to the letter of grace given to him by the valiant Lord residing in Madurai Alavai, and his desire to worship with love, decided to accompany him. At the very moment Sundaramoorthy Swamigal thought of going to Madurai and other sacred places to worship, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar desired to remain with Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, inseparable from him. Moreover, divine grace prompted a thought in Cheraman Peruman Nayanar's heart to go and worship with love the Lord residing in Madurai Thiru Alavai, who had given him a letter of grace, due to his increasing love. Therefore, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar decided to accompany Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. Cheraman Peruman Nayanar had undertaken this sacred journey with two intentions: to see and worship the Lord who had given him the sound of the anklet in Thillai, and to worship Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, who had been brought to his mind by the Lord of Thillai. Here, both these objectives were fulfilled. Nevertheless, he decides to worship many sacred places with Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and go to Madurai. There are two reasons for this. The first reason is to always be with Sundaramoorthy Swamigal. The other reason is to worship the Lord of Alavai, who had given him a letter of grace. For these two reasons, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar also accompanies him. 83rd verse. When the hearts of both were thus united, they arose from Thiruvarur, entered the sacred temple, worshipped the Lord's lotus feet, and by His gracious consent, set out. Their numerous devoted attendants, with their boundless services, vehicles, and treasures, accompanied them. As the hearts of both were united as mentioned before, desiring to go on a sacred pilgrimage, they arose from the sacred mansion, entered the sacred temple of the Lord who resides in the anthill in Thiruvarur, worshipped His lotus-like feet, and having received the gracious consent of Thyagaraja Peruman, they came out and began this sacred journey. The devoted people who always surrounded Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, the servants who were privileged to attend to their sacred bodies, the bodyguards, and other attendants, all set out with them. The attendants who carried their belongings, vehicles, and treasures accompanied them. As these two great individuals were embarking on a long pilgrimage, these attendants carried all the necessary items for them. 84th verse. Surrounded by their serving attendants, they worshipped Thiruvarur, crossed the groves of the outer garden, and reached Keezhvelur. There, they worshipped the feet of the Lord, then passed through the sea-coast groves, and entered Nagai, which shone with flowers, and worshipped Karonam. Surrounded by all their serving attendants, who worshipped them, that is, the attendants who served Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, they set out from the city of Thiruvarur and reached the city limits. That is the eastern limit of the city of Thiruvarur. Upon reaching the city limits, they worshipped the sacred place of Thiruvarur itself. After worshipping Thiruvarur, they crossed the groves of the outer garden, which were full of trees, and reached Thirukeezhvelur. There, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar worshipped the feet of the Lord. Keezhvelur is located a short distance east of Thiruvarur. Although Sundaramoorthy Swamigal visited this place and praised the Lord, the sacred hymn composed by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal for this place is not available to us due to our lack of merit. Thus, after worshipping Thirukeezhvelur and receiving the Lord's gracious permission, they set out from Thirukeezhvelur and reached Thirunagappattinam, which was surrounded by sea-coast groves and shone with abundant flowers, and worshipped the Thirukaronam temple. Nagai is the name of the town. Karonam is the name of the temple. 85th verse. The Chera and Aroorar, having offered the garland of pure Tamil words, which melted the hearts of the devotees, saying, "You reside in Thirukaronam," stayed there for a few days. Then, worshipping other places where the Lord with the matted locks, adorned with the great river, resided, they reached Thirumaraikkadu, the sacred forest of the Lord, the cause of grace. Sundaramoorthy Swamigal went to Thirunagappattinam and worshipped the Lord, composing a sacred hymn. It was a sacred hymn with the refrain "You reside in Nagai Karonam." Sundaramoorthy Swamigal composed this sacred hymn, a garland of pure Tamil words, which arose from his eagerness to melt the hearts of the devotees. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman's reference here to "Thirukaronaththu iruppire" (You reside in Thirukaronam) refers to the meaning of the refrain "Kadal Nagai Karonam mevi iruppire" (You reside in Nagai Karonam by the sea). This sacred hymn is composed with eagerness to melt the hearts of devotees. It is a sacred hymn overflowing with love and good qualities. Such a sacred hymn for the sacred place of Thirunagappattinam, composed by Sundaramoorthy Swamigal, is the 46th sacred hymn in the 7th Thirumurai, set to the tune of Kollikkauvanam. The first and last verses of this sacred hymn are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "You who reside in many towns, singing many hymns, speaking harshly to women, playing tricks, and wandering about, wearing the bones of the dead, riding a bull, you have hidden your wealth. Have no pity on me for a day. Grant me pearl necklaces, shining jeweled diamond chains, and fragrant musk paste that is sweet to the body. You reside in Kadal Nagai Karonam." "O Lord, who is the support for me and the birds with many-voiced languages, who else do I have? You, who are immersed in me, must remove my suffering. I need shining pearl ornaments, nine kinds of flowers, and fragrant musk paste that is sweet to the eyes. You reside in Kadal Nagai Karonam." These are the rare Tamil words spoken by Nanjavur Arooran, who is close to the Lord. Those who master these will rule the celestial world. Thiruchitrambalam. "I am your servant, who else do I have? You must remove my suffering and grant me joy. You reside in Kadal Nagai Karonam." This is a sacred hymn sung with friendship. The Lord is Kayarohaneswara, and the Goddess is Nilayathakshi.

We have already discussed the details of the sacred place of Thirunagappattinam in the Thirupuranam of Thirunavukkarasu Swamigal. Thus, after worshipping Thirunagappattinam and receiving the Lord's gracious permission, Sundaramoorthy Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar set out from Thirunagappattinam and visited places like Poigainallur, Thiruvarinjaiyur, Thirukillikudi, Thirukozhili, Thiruvaimur, Thirumugathalai, and then reached Thirumaraikkadu, the sacred forest of the Lord, which is praised as Vedharanyam. We will continue to reflect. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 17 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 953, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarga. The Puranam of Kazharitrarivar Nayanar. Sundaramurthi Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar arrive at the sacred site of Vedharanyam. The 86th verse. "Munneer valam kol maraikkattu mudalvar koyil senru irainji senneer vaaimai thirunavukkarasu pugali sivakkandrum anneer thirakka adaikka ena paadum thiruvayilai anainthu nanneer pozhiyum vizhiyinarai nayanmaarai ninainthu irainji" is the verse. "Munneer" refers to the sea. It is called Munneer because it possesses the three qualities of creation, preservation, and destruction. It is also said to have three types of water: river water, spring water, and other waters. Munneer also means the primordial water. Thirumaraikkadu is a sacred place surrounded by such a primordial sea. Sundaramurthi Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar go and worship the temple of the Lord enshrined in Thirumaraikkadu. Thirunavukkarasu, who possessed pure truthfulness, and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman, the divine child who incarnated in Sirkazhi, approach the sacred entrance where the divine doors were sung to open and close. Thirunavukkarasar Peruman is praised in this verse as "Senneer Vaaimai Thirunavukkarasu." "Senneer" refers to the quality of purity, which is the nature of Siva. That is called purity. "Vaaimai" refers to the quality of truth, which means the qualities of constantly thinking of and speaking about Lord Shiva. Thirunavukkarasu Peruman, the lord of speech, did not deviate from these. Now, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises Thirugnanasambandha Peruman as "Pugali Sivakkandru." "Sivakkandru" refers to Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Pillaiyar is the divine son of Lord Shiva. The divine Sekkizhar Peruman praises him as a "kandru" (calf) because of the way he partook of the milk of wisdom. The door that is directly in front of the Lord, which is called "abhimukham" in Sanskrit, was sung to open and close by these two great saints. Upon reaching that sacred entrance, with eyes overflowing with tears of grace, Sundaramurthi Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, these two great Nayanmars, meditate upon and worship both Thirunavukkarasu Peruman and Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Sundaramurthi Swamigal praises the memory of having worshipped them individually and extolled them in the Thiruthondathogai. Here, he contemplates the nature of immense grace that stood as the object of divine manifestation. He also reflects on the simplicity of the Lord's associated grace. With that thought, he worships at the sacred entrance. The 87th verse. "Niraintha maraigal archiththa needu maraikkattu arumaniyai irainji veezhnthu paninthu ezhunthu potri yaazhaip pazhiththu ennum araintha padigath thamizh maalai nambi saaththa arul serar sirantha anthathiyil sirappiththanave othi thilaiththu ezhunthar" is the verse. The Lord is like a rare gem enshrined in Thirumaraikkadu, where the abundant Vedas performed archana. The Lord is like the Chintamani, granting whatever is desired. Sundaramurthi Swamigal prostrates himself before that Lord, falls to the ground, worships, rises, and praises Him. He sings and praises with the Thiruppathigam that begins with "Yaazhaip pazhiththu." "Yaazhaip pazhiththa mozhiyemmai" is the sacred name of the Goddess of Thirumaraikkadu. "Pannin ner mozhiyal Umai" is the sacred utterance of Thirunavukkarasu Peruman. This praises and refers to the sweetness of the Mother's language. The Thiruppathigam composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal is the 71st Thiruppathigam in the Seventh Thirumurai. The Thiruppathigam for the sacred site of Thirumaraikkadu is set in the Gandhara pann. The first and last verses of this pathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Yaazhaip pazhiththanna mozhi mangai oru pangal pezhaich chadaimudimel pirai vaiththan idam penil thazhaip"

"Thozhilude senru puzhaiththallai nuzhainthu vazhaikkani kuzhaikkurangunnum maraikkade paror palapudai suzhvala vayanavalar vendhan varur vanamulaiyal umai pangan maraikkatai aruran thamizh malaigal padum adi thondar nirur tharu nilanodu uyar pugazhaguvar thame" Thiruchitrambalam. As Sundaramurthi Swamigal thus composed and praised with the Thiruppathigam, Cheraman Perumal Nayanar, reciting and delighting in what he had already composed praising the Lord in his excellent Ponvannathu Andhadhi, rose up. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar delights in reciting and worshipping here the verses praising the glories of the Lord of Thirumaraikkadu from the Ponvannathu Andhadhi he had already composed. Those verses are as follows: "Thuyarum thozhuvum alum sorum thukilum kalaiyum sella peyarum pithatrum nakum veythuyirkkum perumpini koornthu ayarum amarvikkum muri nimirkkum an thovingane mayaru maraikkattu iraikku aatpatta valnuthale." This verse is in the voice of the foster mother. Now another verse: "Kottum silapala suzha ninrarkkum kuppattru ezhunthu nattamariyum kiridikkum padu nakum veruttum vattam varumarunjaranai sellum malarthayangum puttangkirum pozhil suzh maraikkattu aran puthangale." These are the 37th and 47th verses from the Ponvannathu Andhadhi, verses pertaining to the sacred site of Thirumaraikkadu. Cheraman Perumal Nayanar praises and worships with these. The 88th verse. "Ezhunthu paninthu puraththu eithi iruvar perunthondarum silanal sezhunthan pazhanappathi athanul amarnthu thenpal thiraikkadal nanju azhunthu midatrrar agaththiyan palli irainji avirmadhiya kozhunthu valar sengadai kuzhagar kodikkoyil kuruginar" is the verse. As mentioned before, after delighting, worshipping, and prostrating before the Lord in Thirumaraikkadu, these two great saints came out and stayed for some days in that prosperous and cool town surrounded by fertile fields. Vedharanyam is a coastal town, so the place is cooled by sea breezes. This town belongs to the Chola country, so there are also fertile fields watered by the Kaveri. This also makes it a cool place. In those days, it was a seaport and engaged in maritime trade. It excelled in pearl diving, salt pan industries, and so on. These two great saints stayed there for some days. From there, they went and worshipped the Thiruvagathiyanpalli of the Lord, whose throat was stained by the poison of the turbulent sea to its south. However, the Thiruppathigam composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal for the sacred site of Thiruvagathiyanpalli is not available to us due to our lack of merit. Then, from there, they set out and reached Kodikkoyil, which is the last sacred site on the southern bank of the Kaveri in the Chola country, a coastal site. It is the southernmost border of the Chola country, hence its name Kodikkoyil. The Lord's name there is Kuzhagar. These two great saints reached Kodikkoyil and worshipped Kuzhagar. The 89th verse. "Kodikkazhagar koyil ayal kudigal onrum puraththu engum nadi kanathu ulpukku nambar padam thozhuthu ullam vadi kadithai kadarkatru enru eduththu malarkkannir vara padikkadu kalpunarntha parisum padigaththidai vaiththar." Seeing that there were no habitations of people anywhere outside and around the temple of Kodikkazhagar, Sundaramurthi Swamigal entered the temple, worshipped the Lord's feet, his heart greatly saddened, his mind deeply distressed, and with tears flowing from his flower-like eyes, he began to sing the Thiruppathigam starting with "Kadithai Kadarkatru." He also praised in that pathigam the nature of the Lord residing amidst the forests. Unable to bear seeing the Lord alone, without devotees or worshippers, with the harsh sea wind scattering sand, Sundaramurthi Swamigal, with a heavy heart,

composed this Thiruppathigam. Just as Thirukkannappadeva Nayanar, upon seeing Kudumithevar, lamented, "In this forest of elephants, bears, tigers, and lions, why are you alone on this mountain?" as we saw in his Puranam, so too Sundaramurthi Swamigal here melts and sings. Unable to bear seeing the Lord alone, his great mental distress caused tears to flow. The idea "my wicked eyes have seen" becomes the central theme of the pathigam. Sundaramurthi Swamigal has included the nature of the forest being associated with Kali in this pathigam. "Kadu kal" means Durga. "Kadu kizhal" means the mistress of the forest. It refers to Durga, known as Vanadurga. Vanadurga is one of the nine forms of Durga. We see in Thirumurugatruppadai references like "vetri velpor kotravai siruva ilaiyani sirappir pazhaiyon kolavi." She is also a shakti of the Lord. It describes the harsh midday sun, characteristic of the Palai landscape. We have already seen that "Neeli kottangal" and "Palai" are synonymous, and accordingly, Durga also has the name Neeli. "Neeli kottam" is said to be characteristic of Palai. Here, although it is a coastal area (Neythal thinai), its nature has changed, becoming harsh, with the sea wind blowing, leaving no place for people to live, causing distress. Seeing this as resembling a Palai land, Sundaramurthi Swamigal incorporates this nature into the Thiruppathigam. The Thiruppathigam composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal for the sacred site of Kodikkoyil is the 32nd Thiruppathigam in the Seventh Thirumurai, set in Kollipann. The first, fifth, and tenth verses of this pathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Kadithai kadarkatru vanthatra karaimel kudithan ayale irundhar kutramamo kodiyen kankal kandana kodik kuzhagir adikel umakku ar thunaiyaga irundhire." "Maiyar thadankanni panga kangaiyalu mey agaththu irunthanal veridamillai kaiyar valaikkadu kalodum udanai koyyar pozhil kodiye koyil kondaye." "Parur malisuzh maraikkadu athan thenpal erar pozhil suzh thirukkodik kuzhagai aruran uraiththana paththu ivai vallar sirur sivalokaththu iruppavar thame." Thiruchitrambalam. Thirukodik Kuzhagar. It is the 127th sacred site, the last one on the southern bank of the Kaveri in the Chola country. It is known as Kuzhagar Koyil. It is the place where the remaining ambrosia, obtained by the Devas from churning the milky ocean, was given to Vayu, and as he carried it through the sky, it fell to the earth and formed a Shiva Lingam. Later, the Devas came, worshipped, and received ambrosia. The Lord's name is accordingly Amudakadishwarar. Kuzhageshwarar is another name. The Goddess's name is Anjanakshi Maithadankanni. Lord Muruga is enshrined with one face and six arms. The sacred waters are Amuda Theertham and Agnikkadal Theertham. These are special notes about this sacred site. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 18 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 954, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarga. The Puranam of Kazharitrarivar Nayanar. The 90th verse. "Angu vaikip paninthu arulal povar agal sozhanattu arnar thangum idangal vanangip poi pandinadu thanai sarnthu thingal mudiyar thiruppuththur irainjip ponthu senvilangum mangul thavazhum manimada madurai muthur vanthanainthar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, who worshipped Thiruvagathiyanpalli and Kodikkolugar Koyil while staying in Thirumaraikkadu, return to Thirumaraikkadu, stay there for some days, and then, receiving divine permission from the Lord of Thirumaraikkadu, they proceed towards the Pandya country, worshipping all the sacred sites where Lord Shiva is enshrined in the vast Chola country. They visit the remaining sacred sites in the south and west of the Chola country. These include Thirukkadikkulam, Thiru Idumbavanam, Thiru Usaththanam, and other such places. The routes taken by the Nayanmars from Thirumaraikkadu to the Pandya country are the roads via Pattukkottai and Aranthangi. The sacred sites on the way are these places. They visit all these and enter the Pandya country. However, the Thiruppathigams composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal for these sacred sites are currently unavailable to us due to our lack of merit. Thus, after visiting all the sacred sites at the southern tip of the Chola country, they cross the river known as Thenvellatru. This Vellatru river, located slightly north of Aranthangi, was the border between the Pandya country and the Chola country in ancient times. So, crossing that Vellatru river, they reach Thiruppuththur via Kundrakudi. These two great saints worship at Thiruppuththur. However, the Thiruppathigam composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal for the sacred site of Thiruppuththur is currently unavailable to us due to our lack of merit. From Thiruppuththur, after worshipping, these two great saints proceed to the ancient city of Madurai. We saw that Thirugnanasambandha Peruman went northwest from Thiruppuththur, worshipped Thirukkodungundram, also known as Piranmalai, and entered Madurai via its northern border. However, these two great Nayanmars, Cheraman Peruman Nayanar and Sundaramurthi Swamigal, came directly southwest from Thiruppuththur and entered Madurai via the Melur road. It is a city of "Senvilangum Mangul Thavazhum Manimadam," where mansions shine so brightly that clouds appear to crawl over them in the sky. They arrive at that sacred city. The 91st verse. "Cheraman thozharum achcherar piranum panipon aramar parai madurai alavayilil vananga varama vanthanaiya vazhuthiyar manakkadal kura managar kodiththu ethirkondu kodupukkar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal, the friend of Cheraman, and Cherar Piran, arrive with love to worship Lord Shiva at Thirualavai in Madurai. The Pandya king, with great affection, decorated the entire city, went to receive them, and led them into the city. The 92nd verse. "Thennavar kon magalarai thiruvettu munnare thonmadurai nagarin kan inithu iruntha sozhanar annavarkaludan kuda anaiya avarum kudi mannu thiru alavai manikkoyil vanthanainthar." The Chola king of that time had married the daughter of the Pandya king. Therefore, due to this familial connection, the Chola king was already staying in Madurai. When the Pandya king came to receive them, the Chola king also joined him to welcome Cheraman Peruman Nayanar and Sundaramurthi Swamigal. Thus, the Pandya king and the Chola king lead Sundaramurthi Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar to the beautiful temple of Thirualavai. The 93rd and 94th verses. "Thiru alavai amarntha senjadaiyar koyil valam varuvar mun vizhnthu irainji vanthondar vazhiththondar tharuvarai potrisiththu thazhnthu ezhunthu vazhntha thamizh peruvaaimai malar punaithu perumagizhchi piranginar. Padiyeru pugazh serar peruman"

"Anbar misai vizhnthu adiyenai porulaga aliththa thirumuga karunai mudivedu enru arinthilen ena mozhigal thadumara kadiyeru konraiyar mun paravi kali koornthar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal, the staunch devotee, circumambulates the temple of the Lord with red matted locks, who lovingly resides in Thirualavai. Falling before the Lord, he worships Him, praises Him as the one who bestows the blessing of serving Him generation after generation, prostrates, rises, and with great joy, adorns Him with a garland of Tamil poems, filled with profound truth, thereby attaining fulfillment. How does Cheraman Peruman Nayanar worship? The Chera king, whose fame rises in this world, falls to the ground before the Lord and praises Him, saying, "I do not know the limit of the abundance of grace that made me, a humble servant, worthy and bestowed upon me your divine presence." His words falter, his tongue stammers, and he overflows with joy as he praises the Lord adorned with fragrant Konrai flowers. The 95th verse. "Sembiyanarudan sezhiyar tham paninthu serarudan nambiyum munpuraththu anaiya nanniya peruvakaiyal umbar piran koyilin ninru udan kodupoi iruvarukkum paimpon maligaiyil kuraivarruththar panjavanar." As the Pandya king, along with the Chola king, worshipped the Lord, and Nambi, along with the Chera king, came out of the temple, the Pandya king, filled with great delight, took Cheraman Peruman Nayanar and Sundaramurthi Swamigal from the temple of the Lord of the Devas and brought them to his beautiful golden palace, where he provided them with everything they needed and entertained them. He provided food, lodging, and all other necessary amenities. The 96th verse. "Ulam magizha kumbittu angu uraiyum nal udiyarudan kilaroli pun vanthondar tham iruntha idam kezhumi valavanar minavanar valam peruga matru avarodu alavalaviya viruppal amarnthu kalanthu inithu irunthar." During the days they stayed in that city, worshipping the Lord with joyful hearts, the Chola king and the Pandya king, with increasing prosperity, approached the place where Sundaramurthi Swamigal, the staunch devotee, was staying with Cheraman Peruman Nayanar. They conversed with them, mingled with affection, and stayed together happily. The 97th verse. "Annalil madurai nagar marungu aranar amar padigal ponnaram ani marbil puravalar muvarum potha sennavin munaippadi thirunadar senru irainji sol malaikalum saaththi thozhu thiruppuvanaththu anainthar." In those days, Sundaramurthi Swamigal desired to visit all the sacred sites where the Lord is enshrined near the city of Madurai. With the three crowned kings accompanying him, Nambi, the chief of Thirumunaippadi country, reached the sacred site of Thiruppuvanam. Sundaramurthi Swamigal is the Paramacharya, so these three crowned kings followed him. Sundaramurthi Swamigal desired to visit the sacred sites of the Pandya country. Cheraman Peruman Nayanar accompanied him so as not to be separated from him and out of gratitude for the grace bestowed upon him. Seeing and entertaining these two, and since they had arrived in his country, the Pandya king, and due to his familial connection, the Chola king, also accompanied them to stay with them as long as they were in his country. Therefore, this pilgrimage takes place for the sake of Sundaramurthi Swamigal. As Sundaramurthi Swamigal worships the Lord's sacred sites, the three crowned kings accompany him. Thus, they reach the sacred site of Thiruppuvanam. Thiruppuvanam was a very prominent city in those days. Even then, it was a common sacred site where the three kings would go and worship with their respective rights. Just as Koodal Manikkam, a sacred site in the hilly region, is prominent today. The 98th verse. "Nidu thiruppuvanaththukku aniththaga ner sella madu varum thiruththondar manniya appathi katta thedu maraikku ariyaraith thiruvudaiyar enru eduththu padi isaiyil puvanam itho enru paninthu anaivar." As they proceeded directly towards Thiruppuvanam, the devotees who were nearby pointed out that sacred place. Sundaramurthi Swamigal, beginning with "Thiruvudaiyar," composed a Thiruppathigam for the Lord of Thiruppuvanam, who is difficult for even the searching Vedas to reach. In that Thiruppathigam, he praises and worships, saying, "Is this Poovanam?" The Thiruppathigam composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal for the sacred site of Thiruppuvanam is the 11th Thiruppathigam in the Seventh Thirumurai, set in Indalappann. The first and last verses of this pathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Thiruvudaiyar thiru mal ayanalum uruvudaiyarum umaiyal oor pagam parivudaiyar adaivar vinai thirkkum purivudaiyar urai puvanam itho sirin mikappoliyum thiruppuvanam a iruppidama urai van thanai uran uraiththa sonmalaigal paththu ivai paril uraippavar pavam arruppare." Thiruchitrambalam. This Thiruppathigam was sung by Sundaramurthi Swamigal, worshipping the Lord, upon seeing Thiruppuvanam from outside, on the way to that sacred place. The 99th verse. "Senru thiruppuvanaththu thevarpiran magizh koyil muntril valam kondu iraiver mun vizhnthu paninthu ezhunthu ninru paravip padi ner ningi udan panintha venri mudivendharudan ponthu anggan mevinar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal reaches the Thiruppuvanam temple while singing the Thiruppathigam. With the three crowned kings, he circumambulates the courtyard of the temple where the Lord, the chief of the Devas, joyfully resides. He falls before the Lord, worships, rises, stands, praises, and sings. Then, departing from there, he goes with the three crowned kings who accompanied him and stays in that city. The 100th verse. "Appathiyil amarnthu irainji sila nal arurar mupperu vendharkalodu mun madurai nagareithi meipparivil thiru alavaiyudaiyar virai malarththal eppozhuthum paninthu eththi inburru angu amarkinrar." After staying and worshipping in that sacred city of Thiruppuvanam for some days, Nambiyarurar, Sundaramurthi Swamigal, along with the three great kings, reaches the city of Madurai. There, with true and profound devotion, he constantly worships and praises the fragrant, flower-like feet of the Lord of Thirualavai, delighting and residing in that city of Madurai. The 101st verse. "Senjadaiyar thiru appanur thiruvedagam mudala nanjanium kandaravar nayantha pathi nanniye enjalila kathalinal inithu irainji mindu anainthu manjanaiyum mathil madurai managarin magizhththirundhar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal goes to Thiruvappanur, Thiruvedagam, and other sacred sites where the Lord, who wears poison in His throat, lovingly resides. With boundless love, he worships the Lord there with joy, returns to the city of Madurai, and resides there happily. Thiruvappanur, Thiruvedagam, and other such places are located to the north and west of Madurai. Along with these places, he also worshipped other places to the north and west of Madurai, and also nearby places like Thiruvathavur and Solaimalai, and then returned to Madurai. Thus, the divine Sekkizhar Peruman shows us that Sundaramurthi Swamigal did not go far from Madurai but visited the nearby sacred sites and then returned to Madurai. Let us continue to contemplate. Thiruchitrambalam.

Chapter 19 — Kazharitrarivar Nayanar

Thiruchitrambalam. Periyapuranam, Part 955, composed by the divine Sekkizhar Peruman. Varkonda Vanamulaiyal Sarga. The Puranam of Kazharitrarivar Nayanar. The 102nd verse. "Paramar thirupparankundril senru parthiparodum purameriththar koyil valam kondu pugunthu ul irainji siramali malaichchadaiyar thiruvadi keezh at seyyum arumai ninainthu anjuthum enru arurar paduvar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal, with the three kings, goes to Thirupparankundram of the Lord. There, he circumambulates the temple of the Lord who burnt the three cities, enters, and worships within. "Parthipar" mentioned in this verse refers to kings. "Parthipar" is commonly used to refer to kings who rule the earth (Prithvi) as their possession. Thus, Sundaramurthi Swamigal goes to Thirupparankundram with the three kings and worships. There, he worships the Lord who has a garland of skulls (siramali malai) on his head. He composes a pathigam with the sentiment of "anjuthum" (we fear), contemplating the difficulty of serving at the Lord's feet. "Anjuthum" is the central theme of this pathigam. "Adigal umakku at seyya anjuthume" is the refrain for the Thirupparankundram Thiruppathigam, composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal. In the concluding verse of this pathigam, Sundaramurthi Swamigal states that "Adigal umakku at seyya anjuthum enru amarar perumanai aruran anji mozhintha" is indeed the theme of the pathigam. "Anjuthum" does not mean fear in the ordinary sense. It is a praise, contemplating the preciousness of being a servant to the Lord. Thus sings Sundaramurthi Swamigal. What is that Thiruppathigam? The divine Sekkizhar Peruman explains in the next verse, the 103rd verse. "Koththittai enru eduththu kothil thiruppathiga isai murththiyar thamai vanangi mukkokkal udan munbe eththiya van thamizh malai in isai padi paravi saaththinar sangaranar thangu thirupparankundril." Sundaramurthi Swamigal begins the Thiruppathigam with "Koththittai." "Koththittaiyum" is the beginning of the Thiruppathigam. Starting with "Koththittaiyum," he worships Lord Shiva with flawless musical verses, and before the three kings, he sings and praises with a rich garland of Tamil poems in sweet melodies. Sundaramurthi Swamigal offers this Tamil garland to the Lord in Thirupparankundram, where Shankaranar resides. We saw that "Koththittai" is the beginning of the pathigam. "Koththittai" means "Ko" (param, supreme) and "Thittai" (mountain, hill). So, "Koththittai" means Parankundram. This Thiruppathigam worships the Lord by expressing "anjuthume" (we fear) to serve at the feet of the Lord of Parankundram. This Thiruppathigam was sung by Sundaramurthi Swamigal, addressing the Lord, in front of the three great Tamil kings who stood listening. The Thiruppathigam composed by Sundaramurthi Swamigal for the sacred site of Thirupparankundram is the second Thiruppathigam in the Seventh Thirumurai. It is set in Indalappann. The first and last verses of this pathigam are as follows: Thiruchitrambalam. "Koththittaiyum kovalum koyil kondir ummaik kondu uzhalginrador kollaich chillaich cheththittuk kuththith theruve thiriyum sil puthamum nirum thisai thisai ena soththittu vinnor palarum thozhum araikkovanaththodu oru thol pudai suzhnthu arththittathum pambu kaikondathum pambadigel umakku at seyya anjuthume." "Adigal umakku at seyya anjuthum enru amarar perumanai aruran anji mudiyal ulaganda muvendar munne mozhintharum or nankumor onrinaiyum padiyai ivai katru valla adiyar parankundra meya paraman adikke kudiyagi vanorkkum or kovumagi kulavendarai vin muzhuthu alpar." Thiruchitrambalam. The Lord's name in Thirupparankundram is Parangirinathar, and the Goddess's name is Avudai Nayaki. We have contemplated the Sthala Puranam of Thirupparankundram in the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. The 104th verse. "Iraivar thiruththondar irandu pori arumaiyinai irunilaththu muraipuri mudal vendhar muvarum kettu anji marai munnul mani marbin vanthondar thamaip paninthar. Nirai thavaththor appalum niruththar pathi thozha ninainthar." The three chief crowned kings, who ruled this great world, heard about the preciousness of serving Lord Shiva, and they too feared. They then worshipped Sundaramurthi Swamigal, the staunch devotee, who wore the sacred thread of the Vedas on his beautiful chest. Serving the Lord is not an easy task; it is very precious. Hearing this, these three crowned kings, just as Sundaramurthi Swamigal praised with "anjuthume," also praised with fear. Preciousness refers to worshipping the Lord by contemplating His greatness, our smallness, and His immense, continuous grace towards us, overlooking our flaws and mistakes. It is a reminder that one should not fear to worship the Lord, knowing that He assumes a divine form and adorns Himself with Ganga, snakes, the moon, etc., solely out of compassion for the souls who seek refuge in Him. The three crowned kings also praise the Lord in the same manner. These three kings are referred to here by Sekkizhar Peruman as the "first three kings." Teachers say that the lineages of the three Tamil kings were created by Brahma from the time of creation. The period of Sundaramurthi Swamigal, the time he incarnated and bestowed his grace upon us, is the ninth and tenth centuries CE. We see in history that these three royal lineages were powerful empires ruling the land during these centuries. Such three kings worshipped Sundaramurthi Swamigal, considering him their Paramacharya. Now, Sundaramurthi Swamigal, who possessed abundant penance, resolved to visit and worship all the other sacred sites of the Lord. He desired to visit sacred sites like Kutralam and others further south and southeast in the Pandya country. The 105th verse. "Annattu thiruppathigal palavum anainthu irainja malai nannattu vendharudan nambi tham ezhuntharula minnattum pal manippun vendhar iruvarum milvar thennattu venduvana seythu amaippar thamai viduththar." Sundaramurthi Swamigal, along with Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, sets out to visit and worship the other sacred sites in the Pandya country. The Chola king and the Pandya king both return to Madurai. They also send their retinue to arrange for all the necessities for Cheraman Peruman Nayanar and Sundaramurthi Swamigal for their pilgrimage in the southern country. Sundaramurthi Swamigal and Cheraman Peruman Nayanar set out to worship the sacred sites in the southern direction of the Pandya country, such as Thirukutralam and Thirunelveli. The Chola and Pandya kings, who had accompanied and entertained them until then, return to Madurai. They return to Madurai for worldly affairs and royal duties. The 106th and 107th verses. "Iru peruvendharum iyalbin mindatharpin ezhuntharulum poruvarum sir vanthondar pugazhserarudan punithar maruviya thanam palavum paninthu poi malaichcharal kurumanigal veyil erikkum kuttralam senru adainthar. Kuttralamuthu inithu amarntha kuththar kuraikazhal vanangi sol thama malar punaithu kurumpala thozhuthu ippal mutra velmathi mudiyar pathi paninthu muveyilgal setrar manniya selvaththirunelveliyai anainthar." As mentioned before, after the two great kings, the Chola and Pandya monarchs, returned to Madurai, Sundaramurthi Swamigal, along with Cheraman Peruman Nayanar, proceeds, worshipping many sacred sites where the Lord is manifest. Thus, worshipping, they reach Thirukutralam, where multi-colored gems in the mountain slopes shine like the sun. In the mountain slopes of Kutralam, there are gems that shine like the rays of the sun.

Due to abundant water and dense groves, even during the day, the shade is deep, and the sunlight reflected by the gems helps people to move about. Such is Thirukutralam, a place rich in water and other resources. These two great Nayanmars arrive there. Sundaramurthi Swamigal worships the feet of the Koothar (Dancer) who joyfully resides in Thirukutralam, adorned with sounding anklets, and praises Him by composing a garland of poetic words. Sundaramurthi Swamigal worships the Lord in the temple called Thirukurumbala. The Koothar temple in Kutralam and the Kurumbala Nathar temple mentioned here are two separate temples. The Koothar temple is different from the Kurumbala Nathar temple. We have already contemplated this in the Puranam of Thirugnanasambandha Peruman. Kurumbala is on the north bank of the Kutralam waterfall. The Koothar temple is to its north, on the way to Chitra Sabha. This is very ancient. The Lord of this temple is praised as Koothar in many Thevaram and Thiruvasagam hymns. Kurumbala is the temple where Agathiyar transformed the form of Lord Vishnu into a Shiva Lingam to quell the animosity of the Vaishnavites. Here, the Kurumbala tree is the Sthala Vriksham (sacred tree), and it gives its name to the temple and the Lord as Kurumbala Nathar. "Inithu amarntha" (joyfully residing) refers to the many pleasant resources of this place.
